lined the invitation.\"","One little boy attempted to peep through the curtain, caught sight of his mother in a painted crown, and returned to his seat laughing. Outside, children were producing a rustle with strips of paper for the revised performance. A painted cloud hung over the stage. The music teacher brought a reed flute for a scene in which the captain remembered his first voyage. At the appointed moment they would clap once for an explosion, then stamp for thunder. Bea had abolished the leaking water and the artificial fog. The theater could blend invention and memory without pretending a real danger improved either.","Hal called the officer's inspection an intrusion, then caught himself. The spark of annoyance had come from embarrassment; the check would protect the audience. The band's first tune supplied an immediate uplift to the waiting volunteers. Ellis had given a full statement. Noll denied ordering the damage. His offer of a well-paid contract was documented, but the investigators would still have to establish what had been agreed and what the payment meant. Understandably, Bea disliked the delay. Mara reminded her that a satisfying ending would not become more reliable because they performed it quickly.","The paternity testing kit advertisement was removed from the harbor noticeboard. A new notice acknowledged the purchased testimonials and the limits of the old claims. It did not say that all testing was meaningless, only that this promotion had promised outcomes it could not substantiate. Mrs. Rigg helped revise the display about the captain's child. One letter expressed affection; another gave money. Neither alone established the entire family relationship.","Bea had expected that explanation to bore visitors. Instead, two students began discussing why the letters had been interpreted differently. A theological student pointed out that the sacred text used in the performance had been presented without its setting. Bea offered to replace the passage or introduce it accurately. He offered a brief explanation, and accepted her condition that it contain no lecture longer than the scene itself.","The emergence of so many useful objections changed the program. The early newspaper account had been slanted towards Noll's interests. Bea's own first account was based on assumptions she had now corrected. The mayor could not portray every critic as an enemy of the town now that several critics were helping keep the festival open. Nor could Noll use the contractor's confession to portray the careless rehearsal as perfectly safe. The existence of a deliberate act did not remove the independent need to improve the work.","Mara saved an offline copy of the amended program. Noll's proposed lottery had been canceled, and the remaining budget no longer depended on uncertain ticket income. A final rehearsal began at noon. Cassian was assigned a single line and a wooden sword. A glass jewel in his paper crown caught the light. One boot was too loose; he had to hop back into the dressing room and change it. Mara stood in the wings trying not to laugh. He had survived her evaluation of his tennis technique, but the line about his sacred duty nearly defeated her.","\"If you twinkle like that,\" he whispered, \"I will forget it.\"","\"That isn't a verb you can accuse me of doing deliberately.\"","\"I reckon you'll discover a way.\"","When the curtain moved, the children clapped too early. His confusion was partly due to the interruption, although he generously accepted the blame himself. Cassian entered before the paper storm, faced the wrong direction, and delivered the line to the captain's bust. There was a silence, then a delighted roar. He recovered by inviting the bronze captain to improve the weather. Bea, unexpectedly gifted at dramatic timing, supplied an enormous recorded sneeze.","The resulting boom was nothing like an explosion and exactly like an audience deciding to enjoy itself. Even the inspector laughed.","By evening the harbor had filled. Not with the numbers Noll had forecast, but with enough people to sustain the rowing club and fill Bea's chairs. The decrease from the advertised attendance was real. So was the small uplift in income compared with the previous year. Mara watched Bea resist both exaggerations: catastrophe and triumph.","A boy beside her began to pant after racing from the quay. \"The band needs another player. Can your husband bang the drum?\"","\"His only musical qualification,\" Mara said, \"is enthusiasm.\"","\"That'll do.\"","Cassian accepted the drum. The first slap on its skin set the children laughing; the second kept time. When the chorus began, the sound rose over the boats and reached the houses on the hill. Across the quay, a musician was being exposed to a particularly affectionate child's attempt at the trombone. Ordinary disturbances, such as a missed note, no longer struck Bea as misfortune. No one needed an artificial eclipse to see the little lights twinkle across the harbor.","At the climax, paper stars burst from a box. A few caught in Bea's hair. A gull attempted to steal the cake. Cassian, having reached the limits of his musical duties, gave Mara a gentle pat on the shoulder and asked whether she would like to dance.","\"There isn't room.\"","\"A minor factor.\"","They made room. Her skirt brushed his legs. Around them the audience began to sway, and the stage became simply one more place people wanted to stand together. It was an uplifting end to an episode whose difficulty they had not needed to deny.","Later they walked along the breakwater. The tide was falling. Behind them, volunteers were folding the scenery and checking the floor, work that applause could not complete for them. Mara could hear the faint tick of a boat's rigging and the rustle of her husband's sleeve as he reached for her hand.","\"Was this quiet enough?\" she asked.","\"There was no death.\"","\"You set a modest standard.\"","\"Marriage has improved my sense of scale.\"","She stopped beside the last lamp and kissed him. A boat's wake made a little swell against the stones. In the distance, Bea was telling the band that next year's funeral would be shorter. Winter, she said, had surely learned its lesson.","Cassian laughed against Mara's cheek. They were leaving in the morning, presumably for somewhere less vulnerable to theatrical weather. She did not know whether they would find it. For the present, their fate involved a warm hand, the salt air, and a room where the window could finally be closed against a satisfied town."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1529,"html":"<article Rivals Before Lunch","volume":"婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日","anchor":"chapter-0187","paragraphs":["Mara agreed to the treasure hunt when Rowan said Cassian would be playing against her. Marriage had softened many things in her life. Competition was not among them. She kissed her husband at the breakfast table, stole the last strawberry and informed him that the silver fox trophy would look excellent on her dressing table.","\"I shall enjoy visiting it there,\" he said.","\"You presume you'll be invited after your defeat.\"","A servant dropped a cup and managed to smash it before the taxi arrived. Rowan called this a promising start. The archaeological gardens lay above an old quarry. Their underused terraces had opened for a sunny charity challenge designed to support adult education. A group from Generation Z had written the code-breaking puzzles for several new curricula. They were committed teachers, not mysterious gatekeepers, though Rowan had given each a black cloak. June called the effect \"an epidemic of affordable villainy.\"","\"A marvel of inclusive event design,\" Rowan replied. \"An average Joe can enter. Even Cassian.\"","The admission money would endow a small study fund. Rowan's accountant was booking losses on the abandoned luxury version of the event, which had required imported ice sculptures. This cheaper variation included amounts of tea, biscuits and so on that could actually be consumed. The public guide was already in print. Rowan hoped to liberalize access further next year by dropping the entry fee for local teams.","They were engaged in a friendly game, not a trial of courage at the expense of the staff. Mara was paired with June. Cassian drew Neri, an archaeologist with a slender walking stick and an extremely dry sense of humour. A decades-old notice warned players to avoid closed paths. A dense hedge separated the starting lanes. Where the clues would converge was anybody's guess, and Rowan would not divulge the outcome even when Mara threatened his breakfast privileges. He looked carefree, but a feverish assistant was still carrying signs uphill.","\"You aim to make me lose faith in friendship,\" Mara said.","\"I consider your threat as a compliment.\"","The first clue required an associative leap from a carved leaf to a printed family name. Mara tried not to ascribe every coincidence to design. Neri's scientific notes warned that a decorative mark might be related to a repair, not a hidden message. The same could be true of the scratches below it. Archaeological curiosity did not entitle players to dig in a real historic wall. A spade symbol on a game card was theoretical permission only.","June read the old saying printed beneath it: \"A closed hand cannot catch a gift.\" Mara opened the folded card and found a line inside. \"I aim to reclaim my trophy,\" she said.","\"You haven't won it yet.\"","\"An impending correction.\"","At a fork they were confronted with a choice between a short staircase and a longer garden route. The clear-cut rule was that both led to equivalent clues. Rowan wanted to decentralize the challenge, allowing different routes rather than a hierarchical queue behind the fastest climber. That was humane as well as practical. It would keep an eager player from running over someone slower merely to execute a clever shortcut.","The clue box was filled with paper leaves. Mara chose the stair. June chose to mutter at each step.","A neighbouring team dismissed their choice as a mistake. Its leader Tavin was well-connected and visibly annoyed that nobody had given him a private hint. He regarded newcomers as easy game. When Mara passed, he offered to exchange \"better information\" for her map. She smiled charitably and refused. She was better off with a poor map than a rich stranger's certainty.","Not every smile had to foreshadow a trap. Tavin's did an excellent job. A rumour began to do the rounds that the final chest contained an authentic ancient necklace. That was groundless. The display treasure was a modern gift donated by a jeweller. Real finds belonged under proper stewardship; a game must not intrude on an actual site. Mara had no ethical difficulty with winning a silver fox. She would not devastate a flower bed to do it.","Beyond the fountain, their descent opened into a postwar orchard. A mechanized waterwheel made one revolution at a time, lifting cups toward a little channel. The clue asked them to count the cups, then subtract the empty ones. Mara could go a step further and count the drips. June suggested that this might fall short of sanity. They settled on the intended number and laughed until the assistant could hear them from above.","Cassian and Neri appeared on the opposite path. His coat was open, his hair windblown. Mara felt a pathetic urge to abandon victory long enough to kiss him. She remained mentally committed to victory instead. He seemed to know both facts. His slow smile was an insidious distraction, coupled with the advantage of being shameless about it.","\"When it comes to cheating,\" June said, \"married people should be searched separately.\"","\"When it comes to beauty,\" Cassian replied, \"I claim no unbiased position.\"","Tavin reached the next gate first. He slammed it behind him, an overtly reckless flourish near a loose hinge. A stone fell from the wall. Neri called him back, but he pretended not to hear. They were apt to find real danger if they treated every barrier as a prop. The supposedly endless garden did have boundaries.","Mara touched the new crack. Its length was insufficient to tell her what lay beyond. Moreover, the game card did not mention this gate. Obviously, he thought the restriction was unfair to him. Surely Tavin had taken the wrong turning. She felt suddenly sad for the assistant who would have to explain the damage. Charitably, she could call it excitement. Less charitably, she could see a man who expected every room to open for him.","A crash sounded below the wall. Then came a splash and a very human shout. Mara dropped the competition card and ran toward Cassian, who was already reaching for the service bell."],"questions":[],"wordCount":1007,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0187\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日</div><h1>1. The Wrong Gate","volume":"婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日","anchor":"chapter-0188","paragraphs":["Tavin had found the old irrigation chamber, not the treasure. The wooden landing inside had tilted beneath him and broken at one edge. He was stranded on a dry ledge above a shallow pool fed by a controlled discharge from the channel, gripping a slender pipe that had not been designed to support an adult. The chamber was an archaeological work area, closed even to the garden staff without permission. Its damp smell was no game effect.","Cassian rang the service bell while Neri contacted the site team. Mara would not presume a garden rope could rescue a person. She could, however, lower Tavin a working radio from the staff box. His phone was soaked. \"Stay where you are,\" she said. \"A bad leap can devastate more than your trousers.\"","\"A humane encouragement,\" June observed.","\"I am being charitably restrained.\"","Tavin tried to dismiss the warning as nonsense. Then another piece of landing broke, and he stopped talking. Being confronted with an actual drop improved his attention. Neri kept him from attempting a climb until the trained crew arrived. The access route was dense with stored equipment, so Cassian and June cleared the marked public entrance for the rescuers. A frenzied spectator might be apt to make an unsafe assumption, so Rowan barred the approach. Nobody needed to intrude farther to be useful.","The room above was filled with people. The feverish atmosphere outside made it difficult to think. Amounts of unnecessary advice drifted from the onlookers: use a coat, form a chain and so on. Rowan separated the crowd from the doorway. His carefree master of ceremonies face had vanished. He was not mentally prepared for a rescue, but he could execute an instruction. He would decentralize the remaining garden activities so people stopped converging on one dangerous gate.","June could already hear a rumour do the rounds: the accident had exposed a secret tomb. \"That is groundless,\" she told a guest who wanted a photograph. \"A drainage chamber is not automatically a royal burial because somebody fell into it.\"","Neri gave her a grateful look. In the early planning, the teachers from Generation Z had tried to write curricula that made ordinary infrastructure interesting. Apparently they should have gone a step further and included a lesson on dramatic expectations. A closed gate, coupled with an expensive ticket, did not make a person entitled to a revelation.","Mara considered the ledge as a temporary waiting place, not a guaranteed refuge. She asked Tavin whether he could move his hand away from the crumbling joint without shifting his feet. He could. The pipe seemed to be related to the abandoned irrigation supply. The same might be true of a cable visible below it, but she would not touch it to test the idea. Theoretical knowledge was useful chiefly in reminding her what she did not know.","The rescue crew arrived with proper gear. The impending rescue could not become a race. Moreover, the old landing was decades-old and visibly rotten. Their descent was slow and methodical. A few spectators complained that the delay would spoil the challenge. The old saying about patience returned to Mara in a distinctly impolite form. An average Joe with a stopwatch could not turn this into a safe speed contest. When it comes to rescue, she would rather be bored than impressed.","Cassian found her shaking after the crew took over. He touched her cheek, then pulled her into his arms behind the service door. \"You ran toward the crack,\" he said.","\"So did you.\"","\"Yes. We are both going to be unbearable about it later.\"","She pressed her face against his warm neck and let the shiver finish. Their endless supply of clever remarks was briefly insufficient. He understood the silence without requiring her to explain it.","Tavin came out bruised, embarrassed and alive. His pathetic attempt to blame the clue was contradicted by the guide in print and the sign on the gate. The site manager dealt with his account. Mara did not ascribe his survival to her own intervention; the trained team had done the difficult part. She was committed to telling that story accurately if anyone asked. Mara felt sad for the gardens and furious at his reckless choice. It might fall short of a newspaper romance. It was better than replacing other people's work with a kiss.","The damage would be assessed before the route reopened. Rowan's accountant was booking losses on the cancelled afternoon round, and Rowan accepted that without an argument. He had wanted an inclusive challenge, not a cheap test of who could ignore danger most confidently. He would aim to reclaim the fun without allowing an insidious pressure to win at the expense of care.","Under the sunny awning, players shared bread and water. Neri's family had chosen to immigrate here when she was young. A well-connected visitor had once assumed she was temporary staff rather than an archaeologist. She laughed at the memory now, but the slight was not harmless. Making everyone an easy game for a superior joke could shut out exactly the people an event claimed to welcome.","The ethical next step was obvious: the unsafe section stayed closed. The remaining game could continue in the underused orchard after an inspection. Rowan would liberalize the route order and let teams combine. The new arrangement was less hierarchical and less clear-cut, but it remained fair. Nobody would lose faith in the rules merely because they adapted to an actual problem.","Mara was apt to compete even with a crisis behind her. Cassian saw her eyes lift toward the remaining clue box and smiled. \"Better off together?\"","\"For now.\"","The associative clue on the box involved a pair of carved birds. Their shadows would converge at a marked hour. Whether this would foreshadow another surprise was anybody's guess. June watched them lean over the card, sighed about married people and took the pencil away before they could turn code-breaking into a private sport."],"questions":[],"wordCount":998,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0188\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日</div><h1>2. A Fox for Two","volume":"婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日","anchor":"chapter-0189","paragraphs":["The frenzied morning felt distant. The final round began with six teams merging into three. Rowan insisted this was a revolution in game design. June said it was what happened when a man printed too few clue cards. Mara preferred not to ascribe the whole event to incompetence. June said that if the cake required a mechanized slicer, she would be suspicious. The result was sunny, sociable and considerably less feverish than the morning. She had no intention of letting Cassian hold the pencil, however.","The postwar planting had matured into an excellent maze. Their combined team followed a slender stream beneath the orchard wall. Dense ivy concealed a new wooden bird, installed by the site staff rather than taken from an archaeological display. Its painted wing pointed toward an underused bench. A box beneath it contained amounts of paper ribbon, buttons and so on. Mara could consider the ribbon as a clue because someone had deliberately tied one end through a carved ring. The rest might be decoration.","\"Associative thinking,\" Neri said. \"Useful, until it becomes an excuse to connect everything.\"","Cassian looked at his wife. \"I have been trying to explain that to her wardrobe department.\"","She tied a ribbon around his wrist. \"The connection is now explicit.\"","Rowan had wanted to endow the education fund with a single grand event. A teacher from Generation Z suggested smaller challenges throughout the year. Their curricula could use the gardens without demanding that every visitor become a puzzle expert. They could liberalize participation by offering non-competitive walks too. The proposal was inclusive and less hierarchical than a prize ladder, and Rowan seemed committed enough to try it. He could decentralize planning instead of making every decision himself in a cloak.","His accountant was still booking losses, but the community entry fees would cover the promised contribution. Rowan would absorb his own excessive staging bill. An average Joe was better off not being charged for another man's taste in decorative fog. Charitably, Mara regarded the missing ice sculpture as a gift to common sense. Endless expense was not the same as endless pleasure.","The birds on the ribbon seemed to be related to the pair painted beside the bench. The same appeared to be true of their odd reversed feet. The reversed image, coupled with a date, solved it. It was a simple mirror clue. Mara was confronted with a choice between solving it immediately and watching Cassian turn the card upside down three more times. She chose kindness for almost five seconds. Then she took the card and held it to the polished box lid.","\"A humane intervention,\" June said.","\"I was about to solve it,\" Cassian replied.","\"You are apt to say that,\" Mara told him.","The reflected word named the old greenhouse, where the final clues would converge. They did not need to intrude through a locked door. The public entrance stood open, and a sign in print directed them along the path. They left the fragile beds alone. An impending rain shower might devastate the paper decorations; it did not justify trampling a real garden to beat the weather.","Inside, Rowan had hidden the fox in a tall cabinet. The code-breaking puzzle asked for a date from the fund's first lesson register. She mentioned that her family’s decision to immigrate had begun with a garden photograph like this one. Neri read the inscription while June counted the dots. Their theoretical answer opened the combination. Mara could go a step further and spring the little mechanism, but the proper key was already hanging beside it. When it comes to a game, she did occasionally enjoy taking the offered door.","Cassian lifted the trophy. Mara caught his wrist. Their eyes met, and June declared them both pathetic. Rowan arrived before the argument could become affectionate enough to exclude the room. \"A joint success,\" he announced.","\"A distressing precedent,\" Mara said, then kissed her husband in front of everybody.","The cabinet's second drawer held something unexpected: a birthday cake for June. She had thought her friends had forgotten. The entire secret purpose had been to keep her from working through another birthday. Her suspicion had been groundless. The photographer she had mistaken for a sponsor had been waiting to take their group picture, not turn an accident into publicity. A sad smile crossed her face before it became a happy one.","\"Don't dismiss the cake as a distraction,\" Rowan pleaded. \"I helped bake it.\"","\"That may foreshadow a tragedy.\"","The warning did not prevent her taking the first slice. Obviously, an unbiased cake judge might call its appearance impractical. It was dense, sweet and slightly crooked. The committed teachers and the gardeners received pieces before anyone made a speech. An ethical celebration could exist without becoming an examination of who deserved it most. Surely there was room for both the memory and the cake. The accident had not been erased; its practical consequences would remain. Moreover, pleasure did not require pretending the day had been carefree from its beginning.","A rumour began to do the rounds that June had cried. She denied it, then blamed the cake. Mara made no attempt to execute a formal rebuttal. She reclaimed her husband's hand instead. June could count on them, even when their baking might fall short of elegance. The surprise had not come at the expense of anybody's dignity, and nobody had been treated as easy game. The old saying about friends knowing one too well seemed applicable. What other surprises Rowan had planned was anybody's guess.","They took the remaining tea beneath the awning. Cassian remembered that his borrowed visitor pass would expire at six; Mara asked whether he intended to remain as a permanent exhibit. He said he would require better food. She leaned against him, mentally at rest for once. She would not presume that joy was inevitable. An insidious demand to be happy on command could shut out real feeling. It was possible to lose faith in a plan without losing faith in the people who repaired it.","As the first rain came, he tucked the fox beneath his coat and led her toward the gate. \"Our dressing table?\"","\"Mine,\" she said. \"You may visit.\"","\"Daily?\"","She stood on tiptoe to kiss him. \"I was counting on it.\""],"questions":[],"wordCount":1050,"html":"<article class=\"chapter\" id=\"chapter-0189\" aria-hidden=\"true\"><div class=\"kicker\">婚后外传：银狐寻宝与藏起来的生日</div><h1>3.
